# Spatial Scene VST — Build Plan (v1 vertical slice)

Owner: Maisara · Lead engineer plan · Created 2026-11-04
Source of truth for requirements: [`docs/PRD.md`](PRD.md). Working rules for sessions: [`CLAUDE.md`](../CLAUDE.md).

Each phase below is executed in **one Claude Code session** (two at most) using only its
`docs/phases/phase-XX.md` file, the PRD and `CLAUDE.md`. Every phase ends in a PR that the
contracted DSP/3D engineer reviews. A phase counts as done only when its automated checks pass in CI on
Linux, macOS and Windows, and Maisara has gone through its listening checklist.

---

---

## 1. Tech stack

| Area | Choice | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| Language | C++20 | JUCE 8 baseline; `std::span`, concepts and designated initializers keep DSP code readable for the reviewer. |
| Plugin framework | **JUCE 8** (pinned tag via CMake `FetchContent`) | One codebase for VST3, AU and Standalone; best-supported path for pluginval and auval. You chose it. **Check the current JUCE licence tiers and revenue caps before the first paid release** (Phase 13 checklist). |
| Build | CMake ≥ 3.25 + Ninja, `CMakePresets.json` | Same commands on all three OSes and in CI. |
| Simulator FFT | **PFFFT** (BSD-style licence, pinned) | Fast SIMD FFT on x86 and ARM. Keeps the Simulator library free of JUCE, so it can be tested headless and moved out later. |
| JSON | nlohmann/json (MIT) | Contract serialization in a JUCE-free library. |
| Unit tests | Catch2 v3 | Standard, CTest integration, benchmarks built in. |
| Analysis / reference tooling | Python 3.12 + numpy, scipy, soundfile, **pyroomacoustics** (independent ISM reference), pytest; versions pinned in `tools/acoustics/requirements.txt` | Acoustic metrics (ISO 3382) and independent cross-checks are best done in a second implementation that doesn't share our bugs. |
| Plugin validation | **pluginval** (pinned release, strictness 10) on all OSes; **auval** on macOS | Automated load/state/threading/automation stress tests. |
| CI | GitHub Actions matrix: `ubuntu-24.04`, `macos-15` (arm64; `macos-14` until GitHub retired that image on 2026-11-02), `windows-2022` | Your choice: full matrix on every PR. |
| Sanitizers | ASan + UBSan on Linux every PR; TSan on Linux for threading phases (06, 07, 10) | Catches memory and threading bugs that neither you nor a listening test can see. |
| Generic HRTF | **SADIE II subject D1 (Neumann KU100)**, University of York, Zenodo 10.5281/zenodo.12092466 V2-2, `D1_HRIR_SOFA.zip`, converted offline from SOFA into an embedded binary (≤ ~32 MB, dataset-agnostic header; phase-08 scope 1–3) | Measured dummy-head data under **Apache License 2.0**, verified 2026-11-09 by the researcher on the official record (board decision loop D1, `docs/review/board-smoke/hrtf-head/`; accepted by Maisara 2026-11-09); re-read at pin time. Fallback: MIT KEMAR under the conditions in phase-08 scope 1 (declared unmeasured zone below −40°, Voronoi-weighted averages, DF-EQ on by default, below-grid policy as header data). TH Köln KU100 excluded under "unclear licence" (CC BY-SA 3.0 in the paper vs CC BY 3.0 on Zenodo); re-entry condition in phase-08 scope 1. The head is named only in the licence file, `docs/THIRD_PARTY.md` and the public testing record; AX35 freezes the data behind the id. |
| Measured reference IRs | BRAS benchmark (TU Berlin, CC BY-SA 4.0), downloaded in CI by checksum, not committed | The first adopted open dataset with geometry, estimated materials and measured IRs; its authors say the CR rooms "cannot serve as a direct reference" (researcher finding F2, researcher report 9, `docs/review/phase-04-consult/9-researcher-measurements.md`); second source: phase 05d (decider report 11, accepted by Maisara, 2026-11-05; wording corrected by decider report 15, Challenge 1, accepted by Maisara, 2026-11-05). |

### Architecture in code (the contract boundary)

```
src/contract/   SceneDescription v1 types, JSON I/O, validation, versioning   (no JUCE)
src/sim/        Simulator: ISM, tail, HRTF, IR builder, convolver            (depends on contract only)
src/module3d/   3D Module: editable scene model, params <-> scene, presets   (depends on contract only)
src/plugin/     JUCE AudioProcessor + editor, state, threading glue          (depends on all)
src/cli/        render_cli: scene.json + input.wav -> output.wav / IR        (contract + sim)
```

`sim` and `module3d` must never include each other. A CI script (`tools/ci/check_boundaries.py`,
added in Phase 01) fails the build if they do. The only thing that crosses between them is a
`contract::SceneDescription` value, which carries `contractVersion`.

---

## 2. Assumptions (unresolved Expert questions Q1–Q10)

None of these are settled. Each is a **default chosen so work can proceed**. If the Spatial Audio
Expert answers differently, the phases listed under "Breaks" need rework. Sessions must not quietly
replace these with other answers. A change goes through an edit to this table plus a note in the change log (§6).

| ID | PRD Q | ASSUMPTION (default) | One-line rationale | Breaks if changed |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **AQ1** | Q1 contract | **SceneDescription v1** (JSON, semver `contractVersion: "1.0"`): metric units, right-handed axes (x right, y forward, z up); **shoebox room** `[W,D,H]` with its origin at a floor corner; 6 surfaces, each with **8 octave-band absorption coefficients (63 Hz–8 kHz)** and 1 broadband **scattering** coefficient; listener position; emitters (v1: exactly 2, `L` and `R`, each with position and gain); render settings (`outputMode`: `binaural` or `stereoSpeakers`, `hrtfId`, `tailSeed`, `maxReflectionOrder`). Full spec in `phase-01.md`. | It's the smallest description that can drive ISM plus a statistical tail, and it can grow by adding fields (minor version) or adding a `mesh` geometry kind (major version). | 01, 02, 03, 04, 07, 11, 12 |
| **AQ2** | Q2 method | **Hybrid geometric method.** Early part: **image-source method** for shoeboxes, per octave band. Late part: **stochastic noise tail** with per-band decay from **Eyring + air absorption**, energy-matched to the ISM at a mixing time. The result is a full IR, which is applied by convolution. | ISM is exact only for rigid walls (\|R\| = 1); for absorbing walls it is an approximation that holds at non-grazing incidence, with errors near walls at grazing angles (Vorländer §11.3, §11.3.3). A statistical tail is the standard companion, and rendering a full IR means we can compare it directly with measured IRs (NFR-1). | 03, 04, 05, 08 |
| **AQ3** | Q3 materials | User-facing material properties are **absorption α per octave band, range [0.01, 1.0]**, and **scattering s, range [0, 1]**. "Reflectivity" is derived (1−α) and not stored. Transmission/permeability is **not in v1**. | These are the quantities in published material tables and the ones ISM plus tail actually use. Other parameters would be inputs the engine ignores. | 01, 03, 04, 11, 12 |
| **AQ4** | Q4 smooth movement | Rebuild the IR **on a background thread** when the scene changes, with updates capped at **one per 20 ms**. Switch IRs by **crossfading two convolver outputs over 30 ms** (equal-gain for position and room-size updates (placeholder until Phase 10, AX18), equal-power between unrelated IRs: AX18). Because a crossfade runs uninterrupted and a rebuild targets ≤ 50 ms (AQ10; measured W1/W2 times in §0), the interval between successive new IRs is max(20 ms cap, 30 ms crossfade, rebuild time), each rounded up to a whole convolution partition (at 48 kHz: 32 ms for P = 128, 256 or 512 while the rebuild is ≤ 32 ms; at 44.1 kHz: 34.8 ms for P = 256 or 512, 31.9 ms for P = 128; e.g. Windows W2, 41.6 ms, rounds to 42.7 ms at P = 512, 48 kHz). An isolated update reaches the output after any wait for the 20 ms build slot, the rebuild, up to one partition waiting for pickup and one partition of accumulation (slot wait (0 for an isolated request, AX33) + rebuild + 2P; with no slot wait, e.g. 28.5 + 2 × 10.67 = 49.8 ms for W2 on the Linux runner at P = 512, AX31); it then crossfades over 30 ms. Whether the 20 ms cap delays an isolated request or only spaces build starts is settled by AX33 (Phase 07); Phase 10 confirms or replaces it (decider D1 Q2: a minimum spacing between build starts; an isolated request starts at once). The late tail is cached when only positions change. **Offline bounce (non-realtime) rebuilds synchronously**, so bounces are deterministic. | Crossfading convolvers is the standard way to vary an IR in time without clicks. The resulting interval is within Vorländer Table 15.1's translational update time (700 ms for moves > 0.25 m); its 35 ms rotation rows don't apply to v1 (omni sources, AQ8; head tracking out of scope; AX31). Running synchronously offline means a bounce never depends on thread timing. | 06, 07, 10 |
| **AQ5** | Q5 compute location | **Fully local.** No network at render time. | Offline bounce, determinism, NFR-6/7 and DAW sandboxing all argue against cloud rendering in v1. | 07, 13 |

For a fixed IR, after latency compensation, P does not change the output (UPOLS nulls ≤ −129.8 dB at P = 128, 256, 512, §0 row 06; under an update the fade start depends on P, AX31). Halving P costs 1.5–2.1× convolution CPU per instance (3 s IR, host 512, run 42397114233: crossfading Linux 6.3 → 12.6 %, macOS 4.3 → 6.5 %, Windows 2.6 → 5.4 %) and buys no accuracy (NFR-2). Sequencers compensate reported plugin delay automatically (Izhaki Ch. 11); buffer latency is "important in recording, less in mixdown" (Izhaki Ch. 11), and plugin latency adds to input-monitoring latency (host documentation, researcher F9, F10; outside the corpus); PDC misalignment of a bounce applies to any non-zero latency (Izhaki App. A). ### Additional working assumptions (not in Q1–Q10, but the plan depends on them)

| ID | ASSUMPTION | Rationale | Breaks if changed |
|---|---|---|---|
| **AX1** | **Time alignment:** the IR is shifted so the earliest direct arrival (nearest emitter) lands at t=0, plus the reported latency. Relative delays between paths are kept. No Doppler (IR switches crossfade; delay lines aren't interpolated). | A source 30 m away would otherwise put the track 87 ms late, and moving it would shift the groove. Producers expect the track to stay on the grid. | 03, 06, 10 |
| **AX2** | **Level:** a direct path at 1 m equals 0 dB. Physical distance attenuation is kept, plus a user output-trim parameter. No hidden auto-gain. | Physically honest, so moving away sounds quieter, which the brief asks for. Auto-gain would hide the space. | 03, 07 |
| **AX3** | **Listener orientation (A1/FR-3D-9):** yaw-only. The head faces the horizontal centroid of the emitters, stays upright, and has no pitch or roll. If the centroid is within 0.1 m horizontally, the last yaw is held. | Simplest interpretation of PRD A1 that avoids an undefined direction. | 08, 09 |
| **AX4** | **Air absorption** uses a fixed standard atmosphere (20 °C, 50% RH, ISO 9613-1). Users can't change it. For validation only, `render_cli` takes an atmosphere override (temperature, RH), like `--no-air-absorption`; it is not user-facing and not in the contract (Phase 05 uses it to separate model error from atmosphere mismatch). | The PRD excludes *user-controlled* atmosphere, but large halls and outdoor scenes are badly wrong without HF air loss. See flag F6. Humidity moves HF decay times noticeably (Kuttruff Table 6.1; e.g. 8 kHz in a room with V = 10 000 m³, S = 4725 m², ᾱ = 0.15: −11.6 % / +17.2 % at 40 / 70 % RH; an air-dominated room would differ more), so Phase 05 reports that bound; no UI display. | 03, 04, 05 |
| **AX5** | **"Exactly as saved" (NFR-6)** means bit-identical on the same OS/CPU architecture and within −100 dBFS of the residual across architectures. The tail noise seed is stored in the scene. For the Monte Carlo diffuse estimator (AX39, phase 05b), cross-architecture agreement rests on its determinism package D1–D7 (the prototype check C5 ran on one platform; CI compares E_target and the D5 hashes across the three OSes). | IEEE-754 float results can differ across compilers and SIMD paths, so bit-identity across machines isn't achievable for a reasonable cost. | 04, 05b, 05h, 07, 13 |
| **AX6** | **Validation limits:** an emitter must be ≥ 0.1 m from any surface and ≥ 0.3 m from the listener. The room must be 1.5–100 m per side. Supported sample rates are 44.1, 48, 88.2 and 96 kHz. | Avoids 1/r blow-up. The room limits do **not** bound IR length: Eyring with α = 0.01 in a 100 m room gives T of order 160–270 s (100 m cube, α = 0.01: ≈ 267 s without air absorption; with air absorption ≈ 225 s at 63 Hz and ≈ 160 s at 125 Hz; constant 0.161, Kuttruff Eq. 4.12 / Vorländer Eq. 4.32, which prints 0.16; m at 63 Hz from the repository's ISO 9613-1 code, outside the corpus), so only the 12 s cap (Phase 04) bounds it, and renders exceeding the cap are truncated. T30 can only be measured on a 12 s IR when T ≤ ≈20 s (it needs ≥ 35 dB of decay). Reachable from host parameters from Phase 07. | 01, 03 |
| **AX7** | **Source setup in v1 = PRD setup 1:** one stereo track drives two point emitters, L and R. | The user specified a single stereo source for v1. | 01, 07, 11 |
| **AX8** | **Placeholder plugin identity:** company `Makroof`, manufacturer code `Mkrf`, plugin code `Spsc`, bundle ID `com.makroof.spatialscene`, product name `SpatialScene`. | Needed to build at all. **Must be confirmed before the first beta** (Phase 13); changing it after users save projects breaks those projects. | 00, 13 |
| **AX9** | **Geometric limits** (AX6 clearance 0.1 m, emitter–listener distance 0.3 m) are compared with a **1 nm tolerance** (`contract::limits::kGeometryToleranceM`). Ranges of scalar values (α, scattering, gain, dimensions) are compared exactly. | A position typed at the limit (e.g. 0.3 m away) would otherwise be rejected because 3.3 − 3.0 = 0.2999999999999998 in floating point. 1 nm is inaudible and doesn't weaken AX6. | 01 |
| **AX10** | **Emitter ↔ channel pairing** (AX7): ids `L`, `R` and channels 0, 1 must each be used exactly once, but the pairing is free, so L = 1, R = 0 (a channel swap) is valid. Ids are case-sensitive. **Pending a decision by Maisara/the Expert** (raised in the PR #3 reviews); the alternative is to require L = 0, R = 1. | It is what AX7 and the phase-01 spec literally require. Requiring the usual pairing would be one more rule in `Validate.cpp`, the schema and a fixture. | 01, 07, 11 |
| **AX11** | **Decay times of stochastic decays are measured on ensemble-averaged decay curves.** Where an IR (or test signal) contains a noise-based decay, a per-band T30/T20/EDT gate is evaluated with `tools/acoustics/metrics.room_metrics_ensemble` over R independent realisations (different seeds / `tailSeed`s), with R ≥ (σ₁ / (tolerance/3))², σ₁ being the measured single-realisation scatter. Measured σ₁(T30) of white noise × exp at 48 kHz, 63 Hz…8 kHz: T60 0.3 s: 18, 10, 8, 6, 3.4, 2.7, 1.9, 1.3%; T60 1.0 s: 8, 6.7, 4.4, 2.7, 1.5, 1.4, 1.0, 1.0%; T60 6 s: 3.0, 1.8, 1.3, 1.2, 0.5, 0.3, 0.5, 0.3%. Phase 02 applies it to the ±2%/±5% T30 criterion (R = 300/130/48/24 for T60 0.3/1/2.5/6 s). **Phase 04's ±5%-of-Eyring gate (AQ9) must be stated on such an ensemble too** (so must its seed and 44.1/96 kHz T30 gates, which compare two independent ensembles): one tail realisation scatters 4.4–8% at 250 Hz for T60 0.3–1 s, so at 250 Hz a ±5% gate needs R ≥ (8/(5/3))² ≈ 23 at T60 0.3 s (≈ 7 at 1 s). Single-realisation results are still reported, as information. | One realisation of a noise decay doesn't have the nominal T60 in a band: its band energy fluctuates (correlation time of order 1/B), giving a single-realisation decay time a relative standard deviation of ≈ 0.5/√(B·T) (cf. ISO 3382-2 Annex A). No estimator can remove that; averaging decay curves is the ISO 3382 remedy, and it leaves the analysis chain's own bias, which is what the gates are about. Raised in the PR #5 review. | 02, 04, 05 |
| **AX12** | **`render_cli` exit codes:** 0 ok, 1 usage error (bad or unsupported arguments, including `--automation` until Phase 10 and a `--rate` that contradicts the input), 2 invalid scene (each issue printed as `error: <CODE> at <path>: <message>`), 3 I/O error (unreadable scene or WAV, non-stereo or unsupported-rate input, unwritable output), 4 internal error (unexpected exception). | The phase-02 spec names 0, 2 and 3; scripts also need to tell a wrong command line and a crash apart from a bad scene. | 02, 05, 10 (scripts that call render_cli) |
| **AX13** | **DRR definition:** energy within ±2.5 ms of the direct-sound peak (the largest broadband \|h\| in the first 2.5 ms after the onset) over all energy after that window up to the Lundeby crosspoint (+ its compensation); earlier energy counts as neither. Per octave band it is reported only where the band filter keeps ≥ 99% of an impulse's energy inside the window (1 kHz and up at 44.1–96 kHz), otherwise `None` with a warning. | ±2.5 ms is the common choice in DRR estimation (Zahorik 2002) and keeps typical first reflections out. A narrower band smears the direct sound beyond the window; the leaked fraction f biases DRR by ΔDRR = 10·log10(1−f) − 10·log10(1 + f·10^(DRR/10)) dB. The ≥ 99% rule does not bound this bias at high DRR (f = 0.01: ≈ −0.1 dB at DRR 0 dB, ≈ −3 dB at DRR +20 dB). Bands that can't resolve the window give no value rather than a wrong one. Raised in the PR #5 review. | 02, 05 |
| **AX14** | **Per-band levels of band-reconstructed IRs are measured in each band's central half octave** (exact base-2 centre · 2^±¼, rectangular, by FFT: `tools/acoustics/bands.band_energies_fft(x, fs, 0.25)`), where every band filter of the Phase 03 filterbank is flat (±0.01 dB, unit-tested). Full-octave meters are kept for metrics (ISO 3382) but not for checking a per-band gain where adjacent bands differ by more than ~15 dB. | Any finite linear-phase crossover puts its transition band inside one of the two adjacent octaves, and a full-octave meter adds its own skirts: an ideal brick-wall octave reads only ~16.5 dB above its neighbour through the `bands.py` Butterworth filters, and the Phase 03 filterbank (±⅛-octave transitions) gives ~21 dB with a full-octave FFT sum. In the phase-03 per-band check (α = 0.9 above 1 kHz, 0.1 below) adjacent bands differ by 9.5/19/28.6 dB at orders 1/2/3, so a full-octave reading of the 2 kHz band would measure that leakage (+3 dB / +10 dB at orders 2/3), not the material. Measured error in the central half octave: ≤ 0.07 dB. **Part of the full-octave error is in the rendered IR, not only in the meter:** at order 3, 2 kHz, a meter-neutral full-octave FFT sum reads −37.3 dB against −44.0 dB in the central half octave, so ≈ 6.7 dB of crossover-transition energy from the 1 kHz band is really there; a 28.6 dB step between adjacent bands comes out as ≈ 22 dB per full octave. The phase-03 per-band criterion ("via bands.py") is therefore met **under AX14 only** (the Butterworth meter reads +10.2 dB at 2 kHz and +1.0 dB at 8 kHz at order 3). **Pending Expert:** whether ±⅛-octave crossovers (and this measurement rule) are acceptable. Raised in Phase 03; amended in the PR #6 review. | 03, 05 (if it compares per-band levels of sparse responses), 12 |
| **AX15** | **Length of the early-reflection (ISM) IR:** `ceil(last image arrival sample) + 16 (kernel half length) + filterbank.maxHalfLength() + 1` samples, i.e. the phase-03 spec's "last arrival + kernel" **plus the filterbank's ringing** (maxHalfLength ≈ 163 ms at every supported rate, 7 848 samples at 48 kHz). Every emitter's taps are padded to the longest. | Cutting at "last arrival + kernel" would end the low bands of the last arrivals in a step (a click at the end of the IR) whenever band gains differ; keeping the ringing costs ~163 ms of mostly near-zero samples and makes the IR the exact sum of its images. Raised in the PR #6 review. | 04 (where the tail splices on), 06, 07 (IR sizes) |
| **AX16** | **Scattered energy is reported per specular path, not per reflection:** one entry per image with energy `E_noScatter · (1 − Π_s (1 − s_s)^hits_s)` per band at the image's **specular arrival time**, where `E_noScatter` = (gain/r)² · Π(1 − α)^hits · air². So the scattered part is assumed to suffer every wall's (1 − α) of the path (also those after the scattering hit) and to arrive with the specular path. It is energy-consistent: specular + scattered = the path's energy without scattering (unit-tested); for uniform s it telescopes to Σ_i E·(1 − s)^(i−1)·s. The phase-03 spec's "scattered energy removed at each reflection" (per hit, at the hit's time) is the alternative. | One list entry per path is what Phase 04 needs to feed an energy-matched tail, and it keeps the list as long as the image list, not up to 60× longer. Raised in the PR #6 review. The per-path energy accounting is exact for uniform α: specular + scattered = (1−α)ⁿ (Kuttruff §5.2). With uneven α, scattered energy should take the absorption of the walls it actually meets next (Kuttruff §4.5, Gilbert §4.6), so the tail-level bias has a case-dependent sign, by analogy with Kuttruff Table 5.2's decay-time comparison of fully Lambert rooms. The entries set only the tail level; the rendered early part has no scattered energy (AX21). The remaining bias is bounded in Phase 05 by ΔL_b (phase-05 scope 6): ≤ 1 dB everywhere, accepted and the measured bound recorded here; > 1 dB anywhere, phase 05b (B11) switches tail-level matching to the hybrid's propagated diffuse energy (decider D3, accepted by Maisara, 2026-11-05). **Measured in Phase 05e (X-b bound, phase-05 scope 6): |ΔL_b| > 1 dB in 26 of 345 case-bands at 500 Hz–2 kHz** (max +3.83 dB, Kuttruff Table 5.2 layout 4 at s = 1, absorbing floor: AX16 over-predicts the target; +1.59 / +2.34 dB at s = 0.25 / 0.4; BRAS max −1.18 dB, CR3 ls1_mp2 at 2 kHz, −1.47 dB with per-band s; the six Phase 04 test rooms and every CR2 pair ≤ 0.3 dB; the uniform-α s-cases, where AX16 is exact and only the hit-count residual remains, ≤ 0.43 dB). By the pre-stated rule, **phase-05b (B11) switches tail-level matching to the hybrid's propagated diffuse energy**, under the same independent-reference validation, even if early rendering is not promoted (`docs/validation/report-phase05.md` §6a; AX28 positions; count over the three rooms after phase 05f). Under AX36 the switch waited for R1, R2 and Maisara's decision. **Switch decided (phase-05b session 2, AX37–AX46; accepted by Maisara 2026-11-10) and implemented in phase 05h:** once 05h is `done`, the tail level comes from the propagated diffuse energy (AX37 (5.1); estimator AX39) and the AX16 entries remain only for k_real (AX22, AX37 (5.2)) and the diagnostics. **Until 05h is `done`, the shipped tail level is still AX16**, with ΔL_b,tr per case a known deficit (measured table: phase-05b2 §0.1, phase-05b2 §4 item 7). AX16 − long run of the estimator (C16 (g), 1 kHz): +0.02 dB in the closed 100 m s = 0.3 cubes, +0.06 / +0.10 dB in the low-α s = 1 cube, +2.38 / +2.39 dB in the absorbing-floor cube. | 04 (energy matching at the mixing time), 05 (reference hybrid, AX21), 05b (tail-matching switch decided), 05h (switch implemented) |

Vorländer Annex scattering tables vary strongly with frequency, so this loses accuracy at low and high bands (per-band scattering is post-v1 backlog B10). | 12 |
| **AX18** | **Crossfade law by IR relation:** IR switches keep the 30 ms length (AQ4). A switch to a bit-identical IR is not made (Phase 07). **Equal-power** (sin/cos ramps) for switches between unrelated responses: a state restore that changes the IR (Phase 07), the output-mode switch (Phase 09), large jumps (Phase 10). **Equal-gain** (linear complementary ramps) for position and room-size updates, **as a placeholder until Phase 10 sets the law and its gate**. The same equal-power law joins the ISM to the stochastic tail inside one IR (Phase 04: 5 ms centred on t_mix, cos on the ISM, sin on the tail, squares summing to 1), since the two parts are uncorrelated. | Uncorrelated broadband signals add in power (Vorländer §4.3 Eq. 4.14–4.16; cf. §9.2.1). Mid-fade band power relative to equal endpoint powers is (1+ρ)/2 for equal-gain and 1+ρ for equal-power (Vorländer §4.3, Eq. 4.15 cross term), so equal-gain is the closer of the two where ρ ≥ 0.414 and equal-power below; fading trades colouration against clicks (§15.1). **Correlation of position updates** (Phase 07 decision loop): a listener or emitter move δ changes each path length by at most δ, and AX1 shifts the shared tail by τ = Δr_nearest/c ≤ δ/c; for a pure delay τ an octave band of flat noise has ρ_b = cos(2π·1.06·f_c·τ)·sinc(0.707·f_c·τ), sinc(x) = sin(πx)/(πx) (signal theory, not corpus; amplitude and envelope changes ignored). ρ_b ≥ 0.414 needs f_c·τ ≤ 0.170, i.e. δ ≤ ≈ 58 m·Hz / f_c per update (5.8 cm at 1 kHz, 1.5 cm at 4 kHz); larger moves give lower, down to negative, ρ (Δr = 16 cm: ρ_1k ≈ −0.83, equal-gain −10.7 dB mid-fade in that band). Room-size updates change each path r_i by (k−1)·r_i for a uniform resize by k, approximately (0.1 m margin offset). The product documents state no automation speed in m/s, so no band limit is guaranteed. Equal-gain never raises a band above the louder endpoint (P(g) ≤ ((1−g)√E₁ + g√E₂)², Cauchy–Schwarz). **Same-scene steps (phase-07c-1 decision loop, 2026-11-12; FOR MAISARA FM-1).** An IrSet that replaces an interim IrSet of the same scene, with the same `tailSeed`, the same emitter streams and the same fs, is joined **equal-gain**. This covers a saved, held or stage-1 E_b stepping to the final level (AX48), and the AX49 provisional stepping to the exact tail. The early parts are identical. The tails share the band noise up to rounding, because `BandNoiseGenerator::make` draws one PCG stream and output j depends on noise j…j+2h only (from the code). The two differ only by a normalisation gain and by the exact tail past L_p, which fades in from nothing. Over the played window [t_mix,e, L], the share of the exact tail's energy before L_f is f_w ≥ 1 − e^(−13.82·0.312) ≥ 0.986 (AX49 rule), so ρ ≥ 0.993. For level-only steps ρ = 1. Equal-power would lift the mid-fade by 10·log10(1+ρ) ≈ +3.0 dB (Eq. 4.15 cross term). Equal-gain gives 10·log10((1+ρ)/2) ≈ 0 dB. A T60-changing edit, including the AX49 D5 bridge to the exact tail, keeps the existing equal-gain edit law. A `tailSeed` or fs change gives unrelated tail noise and is joined equal-power. Seeds match per output (`tailSeedForOutput`, Phase 08/09). The first IrSet after a restore fades from silence. A restore over another scene's current IrSet, and the output-mode switch, stay equal-power. Decided for Maisara (decider `docs/review/phase-07c-1/12-decider-decision-r3.md` §7, §9 FM-1; interrogator file 13 SURVIVES WITH CORRECTIONS, row 83 applied), open to her veto. `AMENDS her decision of 2026-11-08: "accepted, merge with no ci"` Amended by the Phase 07 decision loop 2026-11-08 (decider round 2, AX18 text; interrogator SURVIVES WITH CORRECTIONS, advocate PASS); accepted by Maisara 2026-11-08 (no vetoes). | 04, 06, 07, 07c-1, 09, 10 |
| **AX19** | **Scatter of EDT and C80 on the hybrid IR, for the Phase 04 sample-rate gate:** σ₁(EDT) (%) and σ₁(C80) (dB) per band are measured in Phase 04 on its hybrid IRs (not on noise × exp) and recorded here. Values: **measured in Phase 04** (`tools/acoustics/measure_sigma1.py`, all 8 bands per room and rate in `tools/acoustics/data/phase04_sigma1.json`, single-realisation `metrics.room_metrics` of the hybrid omni IR, emitter 0, n = 51, tail seeds from 9 000 000), σ₁,44/σ₁,96 at 63 Hz · 1 kHz · 8 kHz for EDT and 125 Hz · 1 kHz · 8 kHz for C80, and the resulting R per rate (the largest over the gated EDT and C80 bands, set by EDT at 63 Hz in every room): `dead_booth` EDT 31.5/29.7 · 11.1/9.8 · 3.4/3.4 %, C80 3.56/2.71 · 1.22/1.29 · 0.36/0.37 dB, R = 4219; `living_room` EDT 23.4/24.0 · 8.4/8.4 · 2.9/3.0 %, C80 2.51/1.90 · 0.85/0.86 · 0.27/0.23 dB, R = 2525; `classroom` EDT 21.4/23.7 · 9.1/8.8 · 3.1/2.2 %, C80 2.08/1.64 · 0.87/0.75 · 0.26/0.21 dB, R = 2291; `club` EDT 17.5/15.2 · 5.9/4.7 · 2.4/2.5 %, C80 1.31/1.46 · 0.57/0.50 · 0.21/0.20 dB, R = 1210; `concert_hall_box` EDT 13.2/14.8 · 3.7/3.8 · 1.2/1.1 %, C80 0.53/0.52 · 0.28/0.24 · 0.14/0.12 dB, R = 886; `stone_box` EDT 11.7/11.8 · 3.0/2.8 · 1.8/1.7 %, C80 0.87/0.89 · 0.26/0.20 · 0.15/0.16 dB, R = 624. These are measurements (NOT CHECKABLE against the corpus). Measured on the six Phase 04 test rooms at both 44.1 kHz and 96 kHz (σ₁,44 and σ₁,96 per band), with n ≥ 51 realisations per room and rate, so the relative standard error of each σ₁ estimate, ≈ 1/√(2(n−1)), is ≤ 10 % (standard sampling statistics, not from the corpus). They set R for the 44.1 vs 96 kHz gate between two per-rate AX11 ensembles: R ≥ 9·(σ₁,44² + σ₁,96²)/tol², which reduces to 18·σ₁²/tol² when the two are equal. EDT within ±2 % (R ≥ 4.5·σ₁(EDT)² when equal), bands 63 Hz–8 kHz as T30 (EDT reported only until σ₁ is measured); C80 within ±0.5 dB, from ensemble-averaged early and late band energies per rate (R ≥ 72·σ₁(C80, dB)² when equal), bands 125 Hz–8 kHz. | AX11 tabulates σ₁ only for T30 of noise × exp; EDT and C80 of a hybrid IR also depend on the early part. C80 is in dB and can be ≤ 0, so its tolerance must be in dB (Vorländer §6.4.3 Eq. 6.7; its 1 dB jnd is stated only for 500–1000 Hz, Table 6.1). Verified by the interrogator (ledger V26, V27; per-rate σ₁, n and EDT bands: interrogator round on E1–E5 and R1–R10, ledger, 2026-11-05). If the measured σ₁ makes R impractical in CI (e.g. EDT at 63 Hz), the gate moves to the nightly job with the same R; the tolerance is unchanged (decider report 8, accepted by Maisara, 2026-11-05). | 04 |
| **AX20** | **Time-bounded image list in the hybrid IR:** the image list Phase 04 uses for energy matching, and the early part it renders, contain every image of any order arriving before t_mix,e + 2.5 ms (the end of the ISM→tail crossfade); later images are represented by the tail. The early part of the hybrid IR is therefore time-bounded and **`maxReflectionOrder` has no effect on the hybrid IR**; it applies only to the early-reflection-only (Omni ISM) IR of Phase 03 / `render_cli --mode omni-early` (`Kind::OmniEarly`, the Phase 03 IR kept unchanged in Phase 04). There is no clamp of t_mix to the requested order's coverage. | **AX22** | **Realised-k switch and ISM cap for the mixing time:** t_mix,e (Phase 04 scope 2) comes from the expected-k_eff rule, which is continuous in position. If at that t_mix,e the realised k_real = (Σ w_i E'_i)² / Σ (w_i E'_i)² of the matching-window entries is < 19 in any of the bands 500 Hz–2 kHz (AQ9's bands), the emitter falls back to ISM-only up to a cap on the **image count** (every enumerated image counts, pruned or not; handover at max(t_mix,e, t_cap,e)), and the tail is matched there only if the window holds non-zero energy. Images with exactly zero energy are pruned, generically (NFR-8). Cap value: N_cap = ν_r(i₀) (Kuttruff Eq. 9.23) for the largest integer i₀ ≥ 10 at which W2 (AQ10) meets 50 ms in the Phase 04 benchmark on the Linux CI runner. If even i₀ = 10 misses, N_cap = ν_r(10) = 1 560 and the miss is recorded. N_cap is a constant in `src/sim`, frozen once recorded here. It is the same offline and in realtime and in every output mode, and it changes only as an intended output change (this row, goldens via update_goldens.py, a §6 row). For scale: Phase 03's rate (4.5–6.9 µs per image) puts the 25 ms per-emitter budget at ≈ 3 600–5 500 images, so the largest i₀ within budget is expected to be 13–15 (ν_r(13) = 3 302, ν_r(14) = 4 088, ν_r(15) = 4 990, ν_r(16) = 6 016; Kuttruff Eq. 9.23), assuming rebuild time ∝ image count (engineering assumption, not corpus) (decider report 11, accepted by Maisara, 2026-11-05). In the 6 000 m³ room, t_cap ≈ 0.38 / 0.53 / 1.10 s at ν_r(10 / 14 / 30), against t_mix,e ≈ 0.12 s (Kuttruff Eq. 4.6, integrated). **Sensitivity, reported only:** Phase 04 renders each of its fallback cases at N_cap and at ν_r(2i₀), with the same tailSeed, and reports the EDT, C80 and T30 differences at 500 Hz–2 kHz in jnd units (EDT 5 %, C80 1 dB, T at the working 5 %). If any difference exceeds 1 jnd, this row records 'cap limits accuracy'. Phase 13 then re-derives i₀ after optimisation. If the difference is still above 1 jnd after that, Maisara decides whether to spend rebuild time on it (NFR-2) (decider report 8, accepted by Maisara, 2026-11-05). Measured value (Phase 04, PR #8, Linux CI runner, release build, median of 10 position-only rebuilds): **N_cap = ν_r(10) = 1 560** (`sim::kImageCountCapOrder` = 10). W2 took 28.5 ms at i₀ = 10 and 56.8 / 60.5 / 65.3 / 71.1 ms at i₀ = 11 / 12 / 13 / 14 (Windows: 41.6 ms at i₀ = 10); W1 took 20.6 ms. Rebuild time is not proportional to the image count (it doubles from i₀ = 10 to 11 for +31 % images; the cause is not established). In the AQ10 room t_cap ≈ 0.38 s at ν_r(10): the mean arrival time of the 1 560th image of any order (Eq. 4.6 integrated); the code takes the N_cap-th lattice reflection in arrival-time order per emitter, not the set of orders ≤ i₀. **Sensitivity (reported, PR #8):** the fallback cases (`asymmetric_materials`, `edge_values`, `medium_room`, `open_field` and the W2 scene) differ between N_cap and ν_r(20) by up to 39.7 jnd (single renders, EDT at 1 kHz in `medium_room`, far above the single-realisation scatter of AX19, ≤ 2.2 jnd), so this row records **'cap limits accuracy'**; Phase 13 re-derives i₀ after optimisation. The jnd values (EDT 5 %, C80 1 dB, Vorländer Table 6.1) are stated for 500–1000 Hz and are extended to 2 kHz here; T uses the working 5 % (Kuttruff §7.6 gives ≈ 4 %). Interrogator: ledger rows AX22-M1…M11 (corpus c1b171f). The switch is a single jump point in source position: across it the IR segment between the two handover times is ISM in one IR and noise in the other, so AX18's equal-gain premise does not hold there and the 30 ms position crossfade dips in that segment: 3 dB at mid-fade for any power ratio; the largest dip relative to the power-linear interpolation of the two segments grows with their power ratio, ≈ 4.4 dB at 10:1, at g ≈ 0.24 of the fade (Vorländer Eq. 4.14); Phase 10 reports that largest dip, its reference and the power ratio, and gates the total output band-energy dip against the power-linear interpolation at ≤ 1 dB, 500 Hz–2 kHz: measured as the maximum dip of the expected instantaneous output power P(g) = (1−g)²E₁ + g²E₂ + 2g(1−g)C₁₂ against R(g) = (1−g)E₁ + gE₂, computed from the two IRs at every ramp sample; the predicted worst case 10·log10(1 − f(1+ρ)/(1+√ρ)²) at g* = 1/(1+√ρ) (segment only; the gate includes the correlated rest) is ≤ 1 dB for f ≤ 0.41 at ρ = 1, 0.32 at ρ = 10, 0.21 for any ρ (Vorländer Eq. 4.14, §1.7; full statement in phase-10, decider report 11 FR-01, accepted by Maisara, 2026-11-05; 1 dB = G's jnd, Table 6.1, stated for 500–1000 Hz, applied to a 30 ms dip by extension); if it fails, Phase 10 proposes a time-segment-split crossfade as a new AX (decider report 8, accepted by Maisara, 2026-11-05). k_real, the realised σ = 4.34/√k_real and per-axis energy shares are reported per emitter and band, not gated. | The expected k_eff assumes Poisson-like arrivals; deterministic shoebox arrivals can fall far short of it in sparse or non-diffuse fields, where the shoebox image lattice is complete (Kuttruff §4.1, example of the rectangular room; the image concept is exact only for boundary specific impedance +1 or −1, otherwise approximate; for the flat room Eq. 4.3) and a statistical tail would erase flutter (§4.2). k_real is not monotonic in t (both the Hann re-weighting and a strong late arrival can lower k = S²/Q; a new entry x lowers k when x(S² − Q) > 2SQ; derived, not from the corpus), so it is tested only at the expected-rule t_mix,e. Per-axis shares are 1/3 each in an isotropic field (Kuttruff Eqs. 5.1–5.2) and only a necessary condition; q_d is not used (Eq. 8.43 reads a 1-D flat-room field as ≈ 0.85); no diffusion-equation fallback (§4.6). | **AX27** | **Noise subtraction in the validation decay path (Phase 05e; decision D-C):** the Lundeby noise-power estimate N̂ is subtracted from the squared band IR before the Schroeder integral up to the crosspoint; the Lundeby compensation is kept; if the EDC is ≤ 0 at any point before its first crossing of −35 dB, T30 is None. N̂, t_c and the late-decay line used for the compensation are taken from the unsubtracted envelope. Applies to measured and simulated IRs in the validation path (`tools/validation`, scope 7 meter checks); off by default elsewhere, so Phase 02–04 goldens do not change. Lowest gated noise level and measured-band admission margin **L_min = 51 dB** (decision 2026-11-06, decider option C with the interrogator diff check, ledger E5-1; vetoable by Maisara). **Test level** (phase-05.md scope 7): the lowest level, in 1 dB steps, with median − 3·SD ≥ 45 dB in every case A condition of the peak-referred Lundeby dynamic range (`dynamic_range_db`, the D7(1) quantity). Re-measured in Phase 05e (session 1, R = 51), 500 Hz, T = 1 s, median / SD / median − 3·SD: 46.65 / 1.27 / 42.83 dB at 48 dB, 48.56 / 1.27 / 44.74 at 50, 49.56 / 1.27 / 45.75 at 51, 50.58 / 1.26 / 46.80 at 52 (`dynamic_range_db` reads ≈ 1.4 dB below nominal); other conditions at 51 dB 47.04–48.64 dB; paired bias with AX27 +0.06…+0.15 %, 0 unsuitable. **Admission:** a measured band enters the gated AQ9 mean and B12 only if its decay-referred noise margin 10·log10(P_line(t₀)/N̂) (`line_margin_db`; P_line(t₀) the mean-square level of the Lundeby late-decay line at the onset t₀, same band filter; N̂ the Lundeby noise mean-square) is ≥ L_min. The two estimators differ: at nominal 51 dB the line margin reads median 48.18 dB, SD 1.99 dB (500 Hz, 1 s), and on measured BRAS IRs it reads below the peak-referred value (e.g. CR2 500 Hz 51.8 vs 59.9 dB). Decay-referred because the bias is set by the noise relative to the decay's initial level (follows from Eq. 8.29 applied to E[g_m²] with N relative to g²(t₀), see the rationale; Kuttruff §8.4 Fig. 8.13 illustrates it, T = 2 s, noise −41.4 dB). That this admission is conservative is outside the corpus: it rests on the bias falling with level (measured with AX27 0.26 % at 48, ≤ 0.15 % at 51, ≤ 0.13 % at 57 dB) and on the line margin reading below nominal; probability that a nominal-48 dB band reads line ≥ 51 dB: normal approx. from the measured line_margin_db at nominal 48 dB (500 Hz, 1 s: median 46.21 dB, SD 1.83 dB): z = (51 − 46.21)/1.83 = 2.62 → 0.44 %; outside the corpus. Option A (test-level rule on the line margin, which gave 57 dB in the 05e run) rejected: non-monotone in level, SD 2.2–3.0 dB, ±1 dB seed-sensitive (ledger E5-4), no measured bias benefit. Reversal (phase-05.md scope 7): if any case A condition fails with AX27, AX27 is not used and measured bands enter AQ9/B12 only at a margin ≥ 60 dB. If any case A condition at 51 dB fails the 0.6 % gate in the 05e CI run, a nominal-48 dB band reads line ≥ 51 dB in > 1 % of realisations, or the ISO 3382-1 text (outside the corpus) requires a decay-referred 45 dB margin: the test-level rule moves to the line margin (57 dB). | E[g_m²(t)] = g²(t) + N (g_m the noisy band IR; noise uncorrelated with g); with x = t/τ, τ = T/(6 ln 10), N relative to g²(t₀), Kuttruff Eq. 8.29 (no noise term) applied to E[g_m²] and split at t_c gives EDC/τ = e^(−x) + N(x_c − x), so the noise integral before the crosspoint is the bias: ≈ +1.7 % at 48 dB, ≈ +0.5 % at 55, ≈ +0.2 % at 60, ≈ +0.07 % at 65 dB (continuous −5…−35 dB regression, −35 dB point on the noisy curve); subtraction cancels it in expectation. With AX27 the residual EDC term is εN(x_c − x), ε = (N − N̂)/N; the expected T30 bias is ≈ ε times the uncorrected bias (prototype: ε ≈ 0.14–0.17). Not a corpus-named method; ISO 3382-1/-2 have no subtraction annex (researcher; Guski 2015 lists 'subtraction of noise' among compensation methods). Prototype (with AX27, R = 51, same seeds): Lundeby dynamic-range estimate (`dynamic_range_db`) ≈ 1.4 dB below nominal, worst median − 3·SD 42.8 / 44.7 / 45.7 / 46.8 dB at 48 / 50 / 51 / 52 dB (500 Hz / 1 s); at 51 dB the other conditions 47.2–48.5 dB, bias +0.02…+0.14 % (≤ +0.14 % at 51 dB), 0 unsuitable. Interrogator D-C, corpus c1b171f (round 2 diff check); decider 2026-11-06 (vetoable by Maisara). Estimators and admission: decider option C with the interrogator diff check, 2026-11-06, Phase 05e session 1 (ledger E5-1, E5-6, E5-7). | 05e, 05d |

| **AX29** | **Reference-hybrid envelope in the analysed IR (Phase 05e, scope 6):** the per-band diffuse energy at the listener, in 1 ms bins of arrival time at the listener measured from emission (patch time + R'/c, Eq. 4.29), is in the same units as the image energies (unit intensity per steradian: a direct path at r gives 1/r², AX2; Kuttruff Eq. 9.24, one common scale for both). | **AX31** | **Real-time IR pickup under work spreading (Phase 06 follow-ups):** the convolver spreads the k ≥ 1 partition multiply-adds of the next block over the host callbacks of the current block; a requested IR is taken at a partition boundary, after any finished fade has retired, accumulates for one partition and enters the output with the next block: its crossfade starts at s0 = (⌊r/P⌋ + 2)·P (`fadeStartSample()`), one partition (10.7 ms at P = 512, 5.3 ms at P = 256, 48 kHz) later for an isolated update than without spreading; back-to-back updates keep one fade per 32 ms at 48 kHz (AQ4); the reported latency (P + 32 samples, AX1) is unchanged. | Without spreading, the callback that completes a partition carries the whole block's work: at a 64-sample host buffer with a 12 s IR it exceeded its budget (dropout risk, FR-SIM-6, NFR-2). Catch-up or whole-first-block computation at pickup would keep the old timing but put a block of one state's work into one callback on every swap (Phase 10 swaps about every 30 ms). The extra partition is far inside Vorländer Table 15.1's translational update times (700 ms for moves > 0.25 m); Table 15.1's 35 ms source-rotation / directivity row and §15's 50 ms total delay for interactive virtual environments don't apply to v1 (omni sources, AQ8; head tracking out of scope); the 35 ms head-rotation row is deferred to phase-08 item 8 (see the phase-06b note below). Decider D1 (2026-11-07), interrogator SURVIVES WITH CORRECTIONS, advocate PASS (ledger rows P06F-*, corpus c1b171f); accepted by Maisara 2026-11-08 (no vetoes). Reversal: a corpus or Expert limit on update delay that rebuild + 2P breaks but rebuild + P meets (then P = 256 or a non-uniform first partition, not the spike). Open for Phase 08: once the head yaw follows the emitter centroid (AX3), emitter moves change the HRTF set; whether Table 15.1's head-rotation row (35 ms; filter content 'new HRTF') applies then is to be checked in Phase 08. Confirmed by the phase-06b decision loop (D2, 2026-11-07; accepted by Maisara 2026-11-08 (no vetoes)): the alternatives would restore one state's block work in one callback on every swap, the spike the Phase 06 spreading fix was shipped to remove (Maisara 2026-11-07, "Spread only", which chose spreading over non-uniform partitioning, not among pickup variants). Table 15.1's 35 ms rows: source rotation / directivity does not apply in v1 (omni, AQ8); head rotation ('new HRTF') is deferred to phase-08 item 8 (AX3 yaw follows the emitter centroid), not out of scope; the extra partition decides that row only for rebuilds in (35 − 2P, 35 − P] = (13.7, 24.3] ms at P = 512, which includes W1 on the Linux runner (20.6 ms: 31.3 vs 41.9 ms), so phase 08 applies this row's reversal clause if the row applies. The 50 ms total delay is §15 text for interactive virtual environments with head tracking (out of scope). | 06, 10 |
| **AX32** | **Bound on finite input samples (Phase 06b scope 3):** an input sample is taken into the convolver unchanged iff \|x\| ≤ **B = 2²⁰ (1 048 576, +120.4 dB re 1.0)**; otherwise (\|x\| > B, ±Inf, NaN) it is **replaced by 0**, in one comparison (`!(std::fabs(x) <= B)`, true for NaN) in the intake loop of `CrossfadingConvolver::process`, before the forward FFT; this replaces the Phase 06 NaN/Inf guard (same behaviour for non-finite samples; the existing non-finite test stays). **Guaranteed:** every FDL bin ≤ 2P·B ≤ 2³⁰ for any IR, so the FDL never holds a non-finite value. **Sufficient for finite output:** 2P·B·max_m Σ_n Σ\|h_{n,m}\| < FLT_MAX (3.40e38), i.e. Σ\|h\| per path < 1.58e29 at P = 512, N = 2 (largest over the 12 test scenes: 90.9; not a bound over all valid scenes; IrSet::create rejects only non-finite taps). **Not guaranteed:** that samples above B are preserved (they are discarded). **Test** (`[conv][engine]`, P = 128, 256, 512, seeded random host blocks, the 5 000-tap noise IR of the Phase 06 non-finite test, input σ 0.2 noise): (a) bursts of an isolated +1e38, an isolated −FLT_MAX and a same-sign run of 2P samples at +1e38 (overflows without the bound: 2P·1e38 > FLT_MAX) → every output sample finite, and −120 dB null (`residualDb`, the phase-06 AC 2 gate) against `renderOffline` of the input with exactly the \|x\| > B samples zeroed: shows the FDL is not poisoned (fails without the bound); (b) from output sample s_last + L + 4P on (no influence of a sample s at or after s + L + 3P; render length ≥ s_last + L + 4P + 4 096), −120 dB null against `renderOffline` of the burst-free original input: shows recovery, valid under any treatment; (c) single samples +B and −B are kept, single samples nextafter(+B, +∞) and nextafter(−B, −∞) are zeroed, each −120 dB against the matching reference (zeroing a kept ±B reads ≈ 0 dB, keeping a zeroed sample ≈ +87 dB, so both errors fail; says nothing about in-range accuracy, which (a) and the phase-06 nulls cover); (d) the phase-06 nulls at unchanged gates and zero allocations in `process` (AC 5). | Float hosts pass values far above 1.0. Izhaki Ch. 11 gives two theoretical extremes: summing a million tracks at integer full scale stays below B (10⁶ < 2²⁰), the ≈ 764 dB boost does not; neither is a working level. B sits 80.4 dB above a +40 dB hot bus; no source gives a largest legitimate level (researcher F17–F20), so B is a product choice inside the window +40 dB … 1.83e33 (≈ 2^110.5, P06B-22, P06B-55), taken low for a large finiteness margin and a small emitted maximum (\|y\| ≤ 1.9e8 at Σ\|h\| = 90.9). Zero rather than clamp: float audio is trimmed at the float-to-integer conversion on the master (Izhaki Ch. 11), so a clamped +120 dB burst would be heard as full-scale output until its reverb falls 104–114 dB (peak taps 0.155 in medium_room to 0.49, the largest over the test scenes), ≈ 1.7–1.9 T (seconds in halls and churches, FR-3D-3; in headphones in binaural mode, FR-SIM-3), while zeroing leaves a gap; the zero treatment also keeps the precedent null valid (P06B-24). Decider 2026-11-07 (phase-06b D3; interrogator P06B-46…56, 67–69; advocate PASS); accepted by Maisara 2026-11-08 (no vetoes). Reversal: a documented legitimate level above 2²⁰. | 06b, 07 (host input), 13 |

A request with no build running and none started in the last 20 ms starts at once; requests during a build or within 20 ms of the last start are coalesced into one build starting at max(last start + 20 ms, end of the running build). | **AX34** | **Unsupported host sample rate:** if prepareToPlay gets a rate outside AX6, the plugin catches the engine's refusal and enters the fallback: output = input delayed by the reported latency (P + 32 samples, 544 at P = 512, at every rate), unchanged (no output trim, no AX32 guard: like a host bypass, so delay compensation stays aligned); the warning flag is set (shown in Phase 11). In the fallback no IR is built anywhere (buildRoomIR throws at these rates): the builder is stopped before prepare (existing rule) and not restarted, reset steps 1–3 and 5 do nothing (step 3 records no submitted generation), offline processBlock does not build, and setStateInformation only validates and stores. | **AX36** | **AX36 (phase 05b, ℓ at a compute limit).** The scope 0 ladder was computed with the reference hybrid alone to ℓ = 1.25 m (`tools/validation/data/phase05b.json` `ell`); the 0.625 m rung needs a 15–49 GB float32 kernel per case (≈ 147 GB with the current dense solver, which also holds the complex64 transform) and exceeds the session machine: a compute limit, not the c·Δt floor. Scope 0's < 1 % halving rule is applied **per quantity**: a quantity may be used at a rung only if one halving changes it by < 1 % in every (A) case (and in every selected BRAS room where `src/sim` would use it). (a) **Window check** (scope 4): the hybrid's E_target (500 Hz–2 kHz) is certified at 2.5 m (2.5→1.25 m ≤ 0.324 %; a* ≤ 0.42 %; the four (A) geometries) and evaluated at 1.25 m; the report prints per case and band the margin 1 dB − \|hybrid − tracer\| and that case-band's 2.5→1.25 m E_target change in dB; a margin smaller than that change flags the branch as ℓ-sensitive (heuristic flag, not an error bound). (b) **Gate (A) per bin and the scope 5 bias trigger: not evaluated** — the gated bins converge at no computable rung (2.5→1.25 m up to 5.36 %; 3.23→3.18 % in layouts 5/6 at s = 1), and halving Δt at 2.5 m changes them ≤ 0.126 % in the three s = 1 layouts (4, 5, 6), where the ℓ change is largest (`ell.dt_check`), so the cause is ℓ. Recorded 'not evaluated (compute limit, AX36)', never 'passes' or 'not triggered', in §0, the PR, AX21 and the JSON. 'Not promoted' rests on gate (B) alone; acceptance criterion 1 is met through its 'not promoted' clause; the tracer-bias criterion and the Definition of done carry the 'not evaluated (AX36)' note. (c) **src/sim:** no ℓ for early rendering. | **AX38** | **Diffuse-solve memory ceiling, build gate and cost records (phase-05b session 2, decision D-T4).** **(4a, `4a-2G3C`)** A working ceiling per plugin instance for the diffuse computation, at the largest AX6 room (100 m per side), at every supported sample rate, with both emitters; phase-13 scope 1 owns the memory budget; customer RAM, comparable plugins and instances per project (K8–K10) stay declared unknown. **(i) Transient memory and the gate.** The transient peak of one diffuse solve is at most **1 GB**; at most **one diffuse solve runs at a time per process**, through a gate. The gate is JUCE-free; a sleeping wait (mutex plus condition variable), with no spin lock, and the builder's priority is not raised to compensate; held only around the solve, never while holding `stateMutex_` or the builder mutex; never reachable from a real-time `process()` (the only build in `process()` stays the `nonRealtime_` branch), guarded by a debug assert and a test that runs one real-time pipeline beside one solving at the gate (the real-time pipeline makes zero allocations and never touches the gate). Three-class service order: (1) synchronous host calls (prepare, restore, `setNonRealtime`, and `reset()`, which can build through `ResetStep3`) are served first, waiting at most for the one solve already running (the premise that `reset()` and `setNonRealtime` arrive on a thread that tolerates that wait and a build is corrected by D-H3 (e): the open check below); (2) offline-render waiters and (3) builder-thread waiters take turns, one solve each: a real-time instance's builder waits about two solves while another instance renders offline, and an offline waiter about two solves, or about 2M with M offline renders in parallel (recorded; offline has no deadline, F20); within a class, arrival order (FIFO), one solve at a time. The AX48 stage-1 (A) solve at a keyless load runs inside the synchronous restore, so it is served in class (1), ahead of background solves (Seat 5's K2 condition; phase-07c-1 decision loop 2026-11-12, decider file 12 §4, §8; interrogator file 13 row P07C1-81). Builder waiters keep coalescing and solve their newest scene; offline waiters never coalesce (each solves exactly its block's scene, AQ4, AX5); a builder waiting at the gate leaves the queue when `quiesce()` is called (the condition-variable predicate checks the stop flag); the offline path takes the gate only after `quiesce()` has returned. Gate order never changes any output (each solve is a pure function of its scene), tested with two pipelines in either order and with one of them offline; the debug assert and the two-pipeline test also cover the `reset()` path; TSan `[pipeline]` with two pipelines, one switching to offline in the middle of a solve. Known gap: under Bitwig's per-plugin sandbox the transient counts per instance; stated in the public compatibility notes until Phase 13 measures it. **(ii) Resident memory:** at most **128 MB additional** per instance kept between rebuilds, on top of the Phase 07 CI baseline (+122.6 MB at 96 kHz in the maximum room, Linux). **(iii) Visibility:** time waiting at the gate counts in Phase 10's IR-staleness metric. The UI 'settling' state is shown on every instance whose latest change is not yet audible: queued at the gate, building, or in its AQ4 crossfade (Phase 10's IR staleness above zero), project load included. Queued and building are the lock-free `dirty_ != handled_` comparison (`ScenePipeline`); the crossfade state is the audio thread's (`CrossfadingConvolver::isFading()`); how it is exposed to the UI, and whether Phase 10's 'IR going live' is the submit or the end of the fade, is phase-10-2's to define (scope 3). How it is drawn (delay, hysteresis, a scene-level aggregate) is Phase 11-3's (Seat 6). `AMENDS her decision of 2026-11-10: "I accept the board's answer by definition."` (AX38 (iii): the "settling" trigger.) **(iv) Records** (filed with 4c in phase 05h; from Phase 08 also in binaural mode, with the gate wait shown separately), with 1, 4 and 8 instances, in the AQ10 room and at 100 m, at 48 and 96 kHz: per-instance staleness when rebuilding together; the time until the last instance is audible at project load; staleness when the instances are automated together (position and room size); staleness of real-time instances while another instance renders or freezes offline under room-size automation; the offline wait, including the M-parallel case; the worst `quiesce()` latency; the worst host-thread wait (prepare, restore, `reset()`, offline); callback p99.9 of the instances playing during an offline render. Each record states the automation speed or rebuild step it was taken at. The 1 / 3 / 10 s marks are escalation points labelled **UI response-time anchors (Card et al. 1991), not audibility limits**; if the 8-instance, 100 m time until the last instance is audible at load exceeds 10 s, it goes to Maisara before the first release. *Record (2026-11-10):* Recorded (CI run 42909003931, 2026-11-10, phase 05h; 8 sequential synchronous restores on one thread, `test_diffuse_records.cpp` L645–651; Linux / macOS / Windows runners, one run each): 8 instances at the 100 m low-α cube, 96 kHz 21.1 / 20.4 / 25.6 s (8.8× / 8.3× / 10.1× the single-instance load 2.39 / 2.47 / 2.53 s; the excess over 8× is unexplained and the 07c condition 7 re-measure prints per-instance restore times); 48 kHz 9.9 / 7.8 / 11.1 s; AQ10 room 96 kHz 7.8 / 5.9 / 8.9 s. The 10 s line fired (decider files 33 and 36 R3, answered for Maisara): a finding of a build that never reaches a producer (AX38 (i) release rule, closed 2026-11-12 with condition 10; the 8 × 100 m / 96 kHz load above 10 s (19 165 / 19 864 / 20 455 ms, files 19 and 20) stays with phase-13 Raise item 3); remedy phase-07c conditions 9 (or 07d if 07c cannot carry it) and 10; re-taken after 07c (condition 7) with, per instance, the time until audible at the interim level (07c condition 5; the saved E_b at project load) and the time until its final tail has settled; phase-13 Raise item 3 applies the 10 s line to the former on the release machines (read on the larger of record (h)'s prepare-first and setState-first totals, FM-10S) and names the latter as a finding if above 10 s. in the 08-first order (AX37 (5.3)), before phase-05h's re-rendered binaural goldens are frozen, or those goldens carry the SE-derived tolerance; W_max stays one constant for all modes). **Seat 1's floor at a gated scene** (decider file 53 §1): at each gated 100 m build of AX38 4c, Seat 1's floor counts as **met** when the achieved SE is ≤ 0.33 dB per emitter and band and at least one of these holds: (a) the pilot SE is ≤ target/3 (0.028 dB), so the VOV test does not apply; (b) the pilot VOV is < 0.1; (c) the final-range VOV is < 0.1; (d) 30 replicates of the shipping rule against a long run give p95 \|error\| ≤ 1 dB. **Escalation.** If, after D-H1's margin or any growth of the non-diffuse build, the re-derived W_max leaves a gated scene where this floor is **not met**, the result goes to Maisara before any range change, with the SE, the VOV and the replicate p95. The starting-state values are constants in the test, per gated scene, with the file-10 provenance in a comment, re-pinned with a CHANGELOG reason whenever W_max or n\*_max is re-derived (Seat 5, file 17 L155–158). **Escalated** (phase-05h §2 item 1, to Maisara with the SE, the VOV and the p95): the achieved SE crossing 0.33 dB, or the replicate p95 crossing 1 dB (1 jnd of G, Vorländer Table 6.1), at a gated scene, or the p95 crossing 1 dB at the reported s = 1 cube. | **AX39** | **Diffuse-energy estimator in `src/sim` (phase-05b session 2, decision D-T1.1, `T1-F*`).** A seeded Monte Carlo estimator with next-event estimation of the propagated diffuse energy (AX37): the ℓ → 0 form of `ref_hybrid`. Lambert emission per Kuttruff Eq. 4.24; the per-reflection split is s(1−α) non-specular and (1−s)(1−α) specular (Kuttruff §5.2, L2505; §2.7); transport over lattice images per Eq. 4.35; gather per Eq. 4.29; AX4 air; the `matchTail` window; unweighted branching, because s is broadband per surface (AX17); specular entries stay exact. One diffuse solve per scene, shared across output modes (Seat 4). **Determinism package:** **D1** no libm in any value that decides a path; the key is (seed, start end, emitter, path, hit, attempt); **D2** fp-contract off (gcc/clang), `/fp:precise` (MSVC), no fast-math; **D3** RNG known-answer test on all three OSes, no `<random>`; **D4** fixed blocks of **64 histories**, independent of the thread count, reduced in block order, no N-dependent stratification; **D5** a face-sequence hash golden for every path set, equal on all three OSes, with the per-case n\* and N beside it (AX41); **D6** stop flag checked between blocks; **D7** N computed without libm, in whole blocks, recorded in the D5 golden; W_max and n\*_max are cross-platform constants (AX41). The thread pool is process-wide, owned with the AX38 gate. **Seat 7:** CI compares E_target across the three OSes, against AX5; allowed and forbidden wording: no "validated method"; no accuracy claim near walls beyond the CS-NW+ cases (AX43) or where both ends are flagged (AX44); diffuse rain named as a method family only. Seed: AX40. Path count: AX41. Tolerance: AX42. Case set: AX43. Near walls: AX44. Resolution rule: AX46. | It gives the validated model with no patch kernel, which is what made the dense form unportable (≈ 3 TB at 100 m, AX36 (c)), so R2 is met across the AX6 range and D-T3 is not needed. Load-bearing (C3, C5, C7, C14): 15 (A) cases in 8 bands, max 0.088 dB, \|z\| ≤ 1.29; at the 100 m cube (f) − tracer = −0.009 dB; the references converge onto it in five small rooms; C5 checked one platform and D1–D7 carry the cross-platform claim; no published validation (RQ-T1-4, §5 B16). Corpus basis: Kuttruff Eqs. 4.24, 4.29, 4.35, §5.2; Vorländer Fig. 11.38. Board decision loop (decider files 21, 25, 29, 34, 37; interrogator files 26, 32, 39: SURVIVES; corpus c1b171f): max 1, sum 18 under Q1 (a); T1-A and T1-B vetoed. Accepted by Maisara 2026-11-10 (ledger P05B2-M-02). | **AX41** | **Path count, pilot and cap of the diffuse estimator (D-T1.3, `N-3W***`, joint rule, block 64).** **History:** h = forward path h of emitter 0 + forward path h of emitter 1 + the listener-started path h, with next-event estimation to both emitters' images; the work of h is all its image evaluations. **Budget:** W_scene = 2·W_max. **Pilot:** histories [0, …) in 64-history blocks (D4); stop at the first block boundary where n ≥ n\*_max or 2·W_pilot ≥ W_scene; the pilot is discarded; if n\* < 1 000 the pilot-floor flag fires. **Final range** (fresh): N_need = max over emitters and bands of ⌈n\*·(SE_pilot/SE_target)²⌉, computed without libm (SE_target: AX42); N = max(N_need, n\*), rounded up to blocks; N_cap = ⌊(W_scene − W_pilot)·n\* / W_pilot⌋, rounded down to whole 64-history blocks; both fixed before the final range is sampled; N = min(N, N_cap); the range is [8 000, 8 000 + N); if N_cap is under one block, the pilot estimate ships, flagged. The stop test and N_cap use 64-bit integers on evaluation counts, with no floating-point division. **Cap hit := N_need > N_cap.** N may fall below n\* by up to the pilot overrun (≤ 0.153 W_scene of work); this is reported as "floor truncated" in the diagnostics, not as a cap hit. **Diagnostics:** n\*, N, achieved SE per emitter against the target (Seat 7's wording), R, VOV, slope (reported only, never flagged), cap hit, floor truncated, pilot floor, both-near, realised work against W_scene. **CI:** escalate on R, and on VOV ≥ 0.1 where the pilot SE > target/3; pre-registered expected flags at the gated scenes, following C16; a list of every fixture or preset where the pilot estimate ships (`edge_values` at the time of writing, D-H3 (b); kept current in phase-05i); the per-case n\* and N in the D5 golden, equal on all three OSes (AX39); at the gated scenes, realised work / W_scene ≤ **1.02**, pre-registered from the C++ measurements at the pinned W_max 3.05 M (measured 0.935 / 0.991 / 0.986 at flutter / s 0.3 / s 1; prototype ≤ 1.009; the count is deterministic, D7) and not changed afterwards (D-H1 (iii), Q-d; decider file 19, interrogator file 21); beside it, W_pilot and W_final per gated scene are asserted **exactly** in the D5 golden (already recorded there); the ratio is the scene-generic guard. **W_max** (board answer Q2a (A) now, (C) scheduled before the first public release in phase-07c; decider files 43, 47 §4 (ii), 51 §4 item 2): (A) = the restore budget (30 s / 14 minus the worst OS build, AX38 4c, AQ10) less D-H1's margin, pinned; re-derived once under (C) from the background budget (phase-07c); never a time cap; a pinned cross-platform constant in image evaluations, **3.05 M per emitter (W_scene 6.10 M), pinned by D-H1 (iv)** (decider `docs/review/phase-05h/19-decider-decision-D-H1-D-H2-r2.md`, interrogator `21-interrogator-D-H1-D-H2-r2.md` SURVIVES): the (A) formula from the C++ cost gives Linux ≈ 2.4 M, Windows ≈ 5.8 M and on macOS no W_max ≥ 0 (2.1429 − 2.3277 = −0.185 s), so the provisional value is pinned as the (A) value, not a formula result, with no margin taken out of it (the gate carries the register of AX38 4c instead); Seat 6's 3 s per emitter is met at 0.136 s per emitter; re-derived once under (C), phase-07c condition 3, with Seat 6's per-scene reading. **Phase-07c pins its (C) W_max only after the AX38 4c record 'offline-bounce time of a 3-minute room-size automation at 100 m, with its ratio to real time' is in 07c's packet** (Seat 8, `5-seat8-D-H1-D-H2.md` L24; Seat 5 L89). Trigger 1's 'any further rise' is a record, not an escalation (D-H1 (ii)): any rise of the achieved SE ≥ 0.05 dB or of the replicate p95 ≥ 0.10 dB above the file-10 starting state is printed in the margin report; only the 0.33 dB / 1 dB crossings escalate (AX38 4c). Phase 12's reopening trigger for capped presets is phase-12 "Hall presets and J1" (decider file 53 §2; only a preset whose U stays raised through the capped branch at every reasonable placement reopens J1; capped presets with achieved SE ≤ 0.33 dB are reported only; **D-H3 (b), H3-F2 (2026-11-10):** the J1 trigger's cause clause ('because the whole window is long') does not cover a capped U on a near-zero total that sits below the −60 dB line (an open field); such a preset is reported with that fact and its cause and does not reopen J1; `courtyard` (four walls, open sky) is tested as a room, not assumed). **n\*_max** (board answer Q2b (i), decider files 43, 51): **1 024** (a multiple of 64, ≤ 8 000, never below 1 000), pinned by D-H1 (iv) from the C++ W1 measurement (diffuse share of W1 for two emitters 7.65 / 9.03 (max 13.98) / 6.35 ms ≤ 25 ms); not re-derived at (C) unless the W1 measurement per output mode moves it (phase-07c §9); if the C++ cost puts it below 1 000, the conflict returns to Seats 1, 3 and 4. | **AX44** | **Near-listener handling and the "approximate" note (D-T1.6, `NW-4C+` with note U).** Per emitter, E = w·E_fwd + (1−w)·E_rev, with w = d_L²/(d_L² + d_E²), d being that end's distance to the nearest surface, from geometry only. One listener-started set (from the head centre) serves both emitters, both ears and every mode; both sets run in the same D4 blocks and are hashed in D5 (AX39). **Diagnostic flag "both-near":** d_L < 0.70 m and d_E < 0.70 m, pre-registered (C13 §4) and fixed before release; it means only that both ends are within 0.70 m of a surface (C14 (d) and C16 (f) show it does not mark a weak estimate across rooms). **User note** (`TailMatch.approximate`), per emitter: **U = (both-near AND (cap hit OR VOV_union ≥ 0.1)) OR (cap hit AND achieved SE > 0.33 dB)**, with cap hit := N_need > N_cap (AX41; N may fall below n\* by up to the pilot overrun, ≤ 0.153 W_scene of work, reported as "floor truncated" in the diagnostics, not as a cap hit). VOV_union is computed over the pilot plus the final range, up to the first 8 000 histories; it does not feed the estimate, so the final range stays fresh (F14). **Note text** (Seat 6 (i); Phase 11 shows it on the settled scene only, in the info view, never mid-drag; wording by Seats 6 and 7): both-near branch: no ±x from the achieved SE; either the measured replicate p95 of its case class from the C++ reported set, or qualitative text: "level unreliable with source and listener this close to surfaces; move either about 0.7 m away"; capped branch: ±x from the achieved SE only for classes whose reported set shows SE ≥ the replicate SD (layout 4 listener_wall fails: 0.68 against 0.89 dB), otherwise the class p95 or qualitative text. When U is raised on an emitter the user did not edit, the note names the scene, not that emitter, as the cause. **Seat 6 (ii), a requirement:** no default placement of a valid fixture or Phase 12 preset may raise U; if one does, the placement is changed, not the predicate. **Reading (D-H3 (b), 2026-11-10; H3-F2):** 'valid fixture' in (ii) and in the reversal means a scene a user can reach as a room or a Phase 12 preset (the plugin default `medium_room`, the Phase 04 rooms, the presets; Seat 6's stated intent). The contract-limits fixtures `edge_values` (1.5 × 100 × 4 m, one absorbing face: Kuttruff §4.3, 'far from isotropic') and `open_field` (one ground surface, five open faces: the §5.1 diffuse-field premise fails) are not rooms; U is raised at their defaults through the capped branch (achieved SE 0.76 / 3.98 dB and 2.39 / 2.12 dB), stays raised at every moved placement (K-b3, file 18) and is true there. **The golden is the list:** U and the five AX41 flags of every valid fixture are pinned exactly in the D5 golden (`phase05h_d5_hashes.json`), so silence at every user-reachable room is asserted by the golden and a new fixture raising U, or a change of the pilot's draw, shows as a golden mismatch whose regeneration needs a CHANGELOG reason; the two contract-limits fixtures are named by the golden's pinned U (which fails first for any user-reachable room whose U rises); the cause line prints the cause (cap / both-near / VOV_union ≥ 0.1) for every fixture whose U is raised; the reversal is not triggered. **Display rule (Seat 6 (i), a note rule, not the predicate):** no note is shown where the tail it describes lies more than 60 dB below the direct sound after t_dir + 50 ms by AX37 (5.4)'s kept check (broadband energy after the direct peak + 50 ms over the peak's squared amplitude) with E_target raised by 3·SE; the rule keys on that measured ratio only, never on a preset category or a scene name (NFR-8); U stays raised in the diagnostics and the (iii) tier; if shown there: 'Outdoor scene: the reverberant tail is more than 60 dB below the direct sound; its level is only roughly estimated' (no ±x, no both-near wording unless both-near fired). Measured (reports test, every Release run, on AX37 (5.4)'s own quantity): `open_field` −85 dB re the direct peak, 25 dB below the line (the rule applies), `edge_values` −30 / −16 dB (the rule does not apply; the shown note carries the cap wording), `medium_room` +7 dB; C1's '> 44 dB to spare' is the matched tail's per-band window energy relative to the direct sound's energy, a different quantity, not compared here, and no margin has moved. **CI report:** for every fixture or preset where U is raised at default, the cause (cap / both-near / VOV_union ≥ 0.1), whether a placement change can silence it (a one-off record), and the tail's energy after t_dir + 50 ms relative to the direct sound (printed for every fixture). **Seat 6 (iii):** U′ = cap hit OR both-near appears only as a labelled diagnostic tier in a Phase 11 advanced view. **Where U fires** (final: C16b at n\*_max 1 024 with the union VOV, and C16c at 8 000, no change; decider round 4's main-text list is superseded): at 1 024 U fires on the n\*_max 8 000 set minus W1 both_wall_0.3 and `club` both_edge_0.5; every case with capped SE > 0.33 dB and every heavy-tailed both-near case at 0.1 m fires; `living_room` both_wall_0.1 (SE about 0.07 dB) is the one good estimate flagged at both sizes; silent: all six Phase 04 defaults and their one-end placements, `dead_booth` with the listener at a wall, the D-T6 small room, the W1/CR4 reference positions, the closed 100 m cubes at s = 0.3, the absorbing-floor cube and the flutter cube. **GA limit:** within about half a wavelength of a reflecting surface the GA tail level ignores the near-wall pressure rise, up to +3 dB at a rigid wall (Kuttruff Eq. 2.50, §8.7). The rise is reduced next to a partially absorbing face and absent at α = 1 (derived from Eq. 2.49's R = 1 premise: no reflected wave at R = 0; not stated in the corpus). The tail there is GA-true, not room-true. The level is measured per band over the tail window (t_mix,e to the end); for a provisional tail it is also measured over the common window t_mix,e…L_f. G and C80 then move by ≤ 1 dB. **Admissible sources in 07c-1:** **(a) by construction:** a saved E_b whose five-part AX48 key matches gives step 0; it is bit-identical within AX5's scope (same platform and build); across builds on one platform, the E_model fingerprint test guards it and any nonzero keyed step is recorded. The synchronous stage-1 (A) solve at a keyless load is the final in 07c-1, so its step is 0. **(b) the AX49 provisional against its exact tail:** the tail-window truncation loss is ≤ 0.06 dB in every band for every valid scene, by construction (decay ≥ 18.72 dB from t_mix,e,max to L_f; Vorländer §6.4.1 Eq. 6.4). The provisional-to-exact step is −10·log10(1 + (1 − f_b)·δ), with 1 − f_b ≤ 0.0136 and δ ≥ −1 (δ: the relative deviation of the realised remainder energy): ≤ +0.06 dB (δ ≥ −1); ≥ −0.5 dB unless δ ≥ 8.97; ≥ −1 dB unless δ ≥ 19. Asserted on the A4 set (several seeds, 48 and 96 kHz): the step ≤ 1 dB per emitter in every band, both windows; a prefix check, identity up to the AX47 (b) step gain: 10·log10(E(provisional − exact)/E(exact)) ≤ −18.27 dB (= 20·log10(10^(1/20) − 1), the difference a +1 dB step of the provisional gives; −1 dB gives −19.27 dB); −24.55 dB (= 20·log10(10^(0.5/20) − 1)) once the reversal sets the bound to 0.5 dB, per emitter and band on the shipped IRs over [t_mix,e, L_f − h), h the noise generator's filter half-length plus the analysis filterbank's, between two renders of one scene and seed (never against a measured IR); and EDT (ISO 3382-1 A.2.1, as `tools/acoustics/metrics.py` takes it) within 5 % at 500 and 1000 Hz on the whole IR (emitter 0) against the exact IR, in every case whose exact band IR has a direct fraction d < 0.5 (d: energy within ±5 ms of the band peak over the band's total; d = 0.5 is Kuttruff Eq. 5.44's w_d = w_r, the mapping to the measured window a project choice fixed before any comparison). Cases with d ≥ 0.5 lie outside EDT's domain (Kuttruff §8.4: the direct sound unduly shapes the decay curve) and print 'not evaluated (outside EDT domain: direct-dominated, d = …)' with the exact and provisional EDT and that band's steps in both windows; no EDT deviation from them enters the count or the worst figure. Each case also prints whether d < 0.217 (1/(1 + 1.9²), ISO 3382-2's d_min ≈ 1.9 r_c in diffuse-field terms, 1.9 rounded) holds, for phase-05d's ruling on the d constant. At least one case is evaluated at each of 500 Hz and 1 kHz (a non-vacuity check, not a quota). | **AX49** | **Interim tail while the exact synthesis is pending (D-C9; phase-07c-1 decision loop, 2026-11-12).** **What it is.** A provisional tail from the same `BandNoiseGenerator`, seed and emitter stream as the exact tail. **Fade start:** **L_f = t_mix,e,max + 0.312·T60_max**, where T60_max is the longest per-band T60 the exact tail is built with, air absorption included, and t_mix,e,max is the latest emitter mixing time, both counted from emission; the tail therefore has ≥ 18.7 dB of decay after its onset in every band. **Fade:** 0.1 s, so L_p = L_f + 0.1 s. **Where L_p ≥ L** (the exact length), there is no provisional: the exact tail is built in its place, with no handover. **Normalisation:** each band is divided by √(E_p,b/f_b), with E_p,b the realised energy over [0, L_f) and f_b = (1 − e^(−13.82·L_f/T60_b))/(1 − e^(−13.82·L/T60_b)); it is never scaled up to make up for the energy past L_p; it plays at the AX48 interim level, with the ebar of the played decay. **Synchronously**, with ISM + matching + mix, wherever no IrSet valid at the current fs is current: project load, first restore, prepare with an fs change (FM-2′). The two emitters' syntheses run in parallel and are tested bit-identical to the serial build. Every load of a scene with a tail therefore starts with a tail (condition 9). **T60-changing edits.** The ISM is submitted at once over the current unit decays (the D5 bridge; Seat 3), then the exact tail follows in the background; there is no provisional on edits; record (h) prints the ISM latency on T60 edits under the bridge, taken after condition 9 lands (Seat 3's bridge condition, review file 14). Position-only edits keep the cached unit decays (AQ4). A restore of a scene equal to the current one keeps the current IrSet. `reset()` follows condition 10. An output-mode switch reuses the cached unit decays and never waits for a synthesis (Seat 4 M). **Handover.** One AQ4 equal-gain crossfade (AX18). Offline renders, bounces in hosts that signal offline, and `render_cli` never use the provisional (AX5, condition 6). It runs outside the diffuse gate. Every submit passes `decide()` under `stateMutex_` with a monotonic (generation, stage) check. **Acceptance:** AX47 (b) on the A4 set; where L_p < L, the decay from t_mix,e,max to L_f is ≥ 18.7 dB in every band, asserted; the L_p ≥ L branch (100 m low-α cube) builds the exact tail with no handover, asserted; the parallel build is bit-identical to the serial one; a `[pipeline]` test shows offline bypasses the provisional; **record (h)**: restores in sequence on one thread, keyed and keyless, at 1, 4 and 8 instances; 100 m; 48 and 96 kHz; three OSes; a T60_max sweep of 2, 8, 12, 20, 35 and 50 s; printed: per-instance time until audible, and the time from provisional to exact;
